\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:\mathsf{emp}\rangle}~\mathsf{assume}~a~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle(\mathsf{ok}:\mathsf{emp})^{(a)}\rangle}
. Suppose
m\vDash\mathsf{ok}:\mathsf{emp}
, so we know that
\left\llbracket\mathsf{assume}~a\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{alloc}}\!\!(m)=a\cdot m
, therefore
\left\llbracket\mathsf{assume}~a\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{alloc}}\!\!(m)\vDash(\mathsf{ok}:\mathsf{emp})^{(a)}
.

\triangleright

C=\mathsf{while}~e~\mathsf{do}~C
. By the Kleene fixed point theorem,
\left\llbracket\mathsf{while}~e~\mathsf{do}~C\right\rrbracket^{\sharp}(T)=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}f^{n}(\emptyset)
where
f(S)
is defined as in
Lemma

E.4
. So, any
(P,\varphi)\in\left\llbracket\mathsf{while}~e~\mathsf{do}~C\right\rrbracket^{\sharp}(T)
must also be an element of
f^{n}(\emptyset)
for some
n
. We complete the proof by applying
Lemma

E.4
.

The remaining cases are for primitive instructions, most of which are
pure
, meaning that each program state maps to a single outcome according to the program semantics. In these cases, it suffices to show that if
(P,\varphi)\in\left\llbracket c\right\rrbracket^{\sharp}(T)
, then
\left\llbracket c\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{alloc}}(s,h)\vDash\varphi
for all
(s,h)\vDash P
by
Lemma

E.5
.

\triangleright

C=(x\coloneqq e)
. Suppose that
(s,h)\vDash\mathsf{ok}:x=X\land\mathsf{emp}
, so
s(x)=s(X)
and
h=\emptyset
. Now,
\left\llbracket x\coloneqq e\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{alloc}}(s,h)=\mathsf{unit}(s[x\mapsto\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(s)],h)
, so let
s^{\prime}=s[x\mapsto\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(s)]
.
Clearly,
\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(s)=\left\llbracket e[X/x]\right\rrbracket(s)
since
s(x)=s(X)
. It must also be the case that
\left\llbracket e[X/x]\right\rrbracket(s)=\left\llbracket e[X/x]\right\rrbracket(s^{\prime})
since
s
and
s^{\prime}
differ only in the values of
x
, and
x
does not appear in
e[X/x]
. So,
s^{\prime}(x)=\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(s)=\left\llbracket e[X/x]\right\rrbracket(s)=\left\llbracket e[X/x]\right\rrbracket(s^{\prime})
, and therefore
(s^{\prime},h)\vDash\mathsf{ok}:x=e[X/x]\land\mathsf{emp}
. The remainder of the proof follows by
Lemma

E.5
.

\triangleright

C=(x\coloneqq\mathsf{alloc}())
. We need to show that
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:\mathsf{emp}\land x=X\rangle}~x\coloneqq\mathsf{alloc}()~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:\exists Y.x\mapsto Y\rangle}
. Suppose that
m\vDash\mathsf{ok}:\mathsf{emp}\land x=X
, so each state in
m
has the form
\dsser{i}_{\mathsf{ok}}(s,h)
where
(s,h)\vDash\mathsf{emp}\land x=X
, so
s(x)=s(X)
and
h=\emptyset
. We know that
\left\llbracket x\coloneqq\mathsf{alloc}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{alloc}}(s,h)=\mathsf{bind}_{\mathcal{W}}(\mathsf{alloc}(s,h),\lambda(\ell,v).\mathsf{unit}(s[x\mapsto\ell],h[\ell\mapsto v]))
where
\ell\notin\mathsf{dom}(h)
. Let
s^{\prime}=s[x\mapsto\ell]
and
h^{\prime}=h[\ell\mapsto v]
. Clearly,
h^{\prime}(\left\llbracket x\right\rrbracket(s^{\prime}))=h^{\prime}(\ell)=v
, so
(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\vDash\exists Y.x\mapsto Y
. Since this is true for all end states, and since alloc does not alter the total mass of the distribution, then
\left\llbracket x\coloneqq\mathsf{alloc}()\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{alloc}}\!\!(m)\vDash\mathsf{ok}:\exists Y.x\mapsto Y
.

\triangleright

C=\mathsf{free}(x)
. There are three cases since specifications for free can take on multiple forms. In the first case, we need to show that
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:e\mapsto X\rangle}~\mathsf{free}(e)~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:e\not\mapsto\rangle}
. Suppose
(s,h)\vDash e\mapsto X
, so
h(\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(s))=s(X)
. We also know that
\left\llbracket\mathsf{free}(e)\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{alloc}}(s,h)=\mathsf{unit}(s,h[\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(s)\mapsto\bot])
, and since
(s,h[\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(s)\mapsto\bot])\vDash e\not\mapsto
, the claim follows by
Lemma

E.5
.

In the other cases, we need to show that:

\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:e\not\mapsto\rangle}~\mathsf{free}(e)~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{er}:e\not\mapsto\rangle}\quad\text{and}\quad\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:e=\mathsf{null}\rangle}~\mathsf{free}(e)~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{er}:e=\mathsf{null}\rangle}

Suppose
(s,h)\vDash e\not\mapsto
and so
h(\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(s))=\bot
. Clearly,
\left\llbracket\mathsf{free}(e)\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{alloc}}(s,h)=\mathsf{error}(s,h)
, so by
Lemma

E.5
the claim holds. The case where
e=\mathsf{null}
is nearly identical.

\triangleright

C=[e_{1}]\leftarrow e_{2}
. There are three cases, first we must show that
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:e_{1}\mapsto X\rangle}~[e_{1}]\leftarrow e_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:e_{1}\mapsto e_{2}\rangle}
. Suppose
(s,h)\vDash e_{1}\mapsto X
, so
h(\left\llbracket e_{1}\right\rrbracket(s))=s(X)
and therefore that memory address is allocated since
s(X)\in\mathsf{Val}
. This means that
\left\llbracket[e_{1}]\leftarrow e_{2}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{alloc}}(s,h)=\mathsf{unit}(s,h[\left\llbracket e_{1}\right\rrbracket(s)\mapsto\left\llbracket e_{2}\right\rrbracket(s)])
and clearly
(s,h[\left\llbracket e_{1}\right\rrbracket(s)\mapsto\left\llbracket e_{2}\right\rrbracket(s)])\vDash e_{1}\mapsto e_{2}
by definition, so the claim holds by
Lemma

E.5
.

In the remaining case, we must show that
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:e_{1}\not\mapsto\rangle}~[e_{1}]\leftarrow e_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{er}:e_{1}\not\mapsto\rangle}
and
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:e_{1}=\mathsf{null}\rangle}~[e_{1}]\leftarrow e_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{er}:e_{1}=\mathsf{null}\rangle}
. The proof is similar to the second case for
\mathsf{free}(e)
.

\triangleright

C=x\leftarrow[e]
. There are three cases, first we must show that
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:x=X\land e\mapsto Y\rangle}~x\leftarrow[e]~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:x=Y\land e[X/x]\mapsto Y\rangle}
. Suppose that
(s,h)\vDash x=X\land e\mapsto Y
, so
s(x)=s(X)
and
h(\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(s))=s(Y)
. We also know that
\left\llbracket x\leftarrow[e]\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{alloc}}(s,h)=\mathsf{unit}(s[x\mapsto h(\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(s))],h)
. Let
s^{\prime}=s[x\mapsto h(\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(s))]
, as we showed in the
x\coloneqq e
case,
\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(s)=\left\llbracket e[X/x]\right\rrbracket(s)=\left\llbracket e[X/x]\right\rrbracket(s^{\prime})
. So, this means that
h(\left\llbracket e[X/x]\right\rrbracket(s^{\prime}))=h(\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(s))=s(Y)
and
s^{\prime}(x)=\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(s)=s(Y)
, so clearly
(s^{\prime},h)\vDash x=Y\land e[X/x]\mapsto Y
and the claim follows from
Lemma

E.5
.

In the remaining case, we need to show that
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:e\not\mapsto\rangle}~x\leftarrow[e]~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{er}:e\not\mapsto\rangle}
and
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:e=\mathsf{null}\rangle}~x\leftarrow[e]~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{er}:e=\mathsf{null}\rangle}
. The proof is similar to the second case for
\mathsf{free}(e)
.

\triangleright

C=\mathsf{error}()
. We need to show that
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:\mathsf{emp}\rangle}~\mathsf{error}()~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{er}:\mathsf{emp}\rangle}
. Suppose
(s,h)\vDash\mathsf{emp}
. Clearly,
\left\llbracket\mathsf{error}()\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{alloc}}(s,h)=\mathsf{error}(s,h)
, and so the claim holds by
Lemma

E.5
.

\triangleright

C=f(\vec{e})
. Any element of
\left\llbracket f(\vec{e})\right\rrbracket^{\sharp}(T)
must have the form
(P\land\vec{x}=\vec{X})
where
(P,\varphi)\in\mathsf{seq}(\mathsf{ok}:\vec{x}=\vec{e}[X/x],T(f(\vec{x})),\mathsf{mod}(f))
. By
Lemma

C.5
, we get:

\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:P\land\vec{x}=\vec{e}[X/x]\rangle}~f(\vec{x})~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\varphi\rangle}

Now, we need to show that
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:P\land\vec{x}=\vec{X}\rangle}~f(\vec{e})~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\varphi\rangle}
.
Suppose that
m\vDash\mathsf{ok}:P\land\vec{x}=\vec{X}
. Let
m^{\prime}
be obtained by taking every state
\dsser{i}_{\mathsf{ok}}(s,h)\in\mathsf{supp}(m)
and modifying it to be
\dsser{i}_{\mathsf{ok}}(s[\vec{x}\mapsto\left\llbracket\vec{e}\right\rrbracket(s)],h)
. By a similar argument to the
x\coloneqq e
case, we know that
m^{\prime}\vDash\mathsf{ok}:\vec{x}=\vec{e}[\vec{X}/\vec{x}]
. We know
P
is disjoint from the program variables by the definition of
\mathsf{seq}
, so
m^{\prime}\vDash\mathsf{ok}:P
as well, since
m\vDash\mathsf{ok}:P
and the only difference between
m
and
m^{\prime}
is updates to the program variables.
Let
C
be the body of
f
and note that
\left\llbracket f(\vec{x})\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{alloc}}\!\!(m^{\prime})=\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{alloc}}\!\!(m^{\prime})
since the initial modification of the program state is just updating variable values to themselves.
We know from
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:P\land\vec{x}=\vec{e}[X/x]\rangle}~f(\vec{x})~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\varphi\rangle}
that
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{alloc}}\!\!(m^{\prime})\vDash\varphi
and by definition,
\left\llbracket f(\vec{e})\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{alloc}}\!\!(m)=\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{alloc}}\!\!(m^{\prime})
, so
\left\llbracket f(\vec{e})\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{alloc}}\!\!(m)\vDash\varphi
.

In addition, we show that the two refinements for single-path computation and loops invariants are sound too:

\triangleright

Single Path. Any element of
\left\llbracket C_{1}+C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\sharp}(T)
has one of two forms. In the first case, we need to show that
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:P\rangle}~C_{1}+C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\varphi\oplus\top\rangle}
given that
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:P\rangle}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\varphi\rangle}
. Suppose that
m\vDash\mathsf{ok}:P
. By our assumption, we know that
\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{alloc}}\!\!(m)\vDash\varphi
. Now,
\left\llbracket C_{1}+C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{alloc}}\!\!(m)=\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{alloc}}\!\!(m)+\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{alloc}}\!\!(m)
and clearly
\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{alloc}}\!\!(m)\vDash\top
, so
\left\llbracket C_{1}+C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{alloc}}\!\!(m)\vDash\varphi\oplus\top
. The second case is symmetrical, using the fact that
+
is commutative.

\triangleright

Loop invariants. We need to show that
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:I\rangle}~\mathsf{while}~e~\mathsf{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle(\mathsf{ok}:I\land\lnot e)\vee(\top){0}\rangle}
given that
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:I\land e\rangle}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\langle\mathsf{ok}:I\rangle}
. Note that this case is only valid for deterministic or nondeterministic programs (not probabilistic ones). Suppose
m\vDash\mathsf{ok}:I
, so every state in
\mathsf{supp}(m)
has the form
\dsser{i}_{\mathsf{ok}}(s,h)
where
(s,h)\vDash I
. By assumption, we know that every execution of the loop body will preserve the truth of
I
, so either all the states in
\left\llbracket\mathsf{while}~e~\mathsf{do}~C\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{alloc}}(s,h)
must satisfy
I
and
\lnot e
, or there are no terminating states. In other words,
\left\llbracket\mathsf{while}~e~\mathsf{do}~C\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{alloc}}(s,h)\vDash(\mathsf{ok}:I\land\lnot e)\vee\top^{(\dsser{0})}
. In the deterministic case, we are done since there can only be a single start state. In the nondeterministic case, each start state
(s,h)
leads to a set of end states satisfying
(\mathsf{ok}:I\land\lnot e)\vee\top^{(\dsser{0})}
, then the union of all these states will also satisfy
(\mathsf{ok}:I\land\lnot e)\vee\top^{(\dsser{0})}
.

∎
